Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Unrestrained Buying Sprees: Spending excessive amounts of money or shopping recklessly to a degree that creates financial distress for self and family.\n  - Foolish Business Investments: Making rash business investments or ventures without sufficient review.\n  - Sexual Indiscretions: Engaging in indiscriminate or risky sexual behavior that is out of character.\n  - Impulsive Risky Behavior: Excessively dangerous or foolish behavior, such as reckless driving or drug use, without considering the consequences.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  Duration: 5 months\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Even though things have been really tough and I feel very down, those kinds of thoughts haven't crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling slowed down or agitated, or has it been hard to concentrate on things during this time?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel slowed down or have trouble concentrating, but during my high periods, I get extremely agitated and restless. I felt overflowing with energy, but I would also get extremely irritated and angry over the smallest things. During those times, I ended up doing some really impulsive and risky things without thinking about the consequences.\n\nDoctor: Did those high periods ever cause major problems like needing hospitalization, losing touch with reality, or being unable to function at work or home?\n\nPatient: No, I never needed to be hospitalized or lost touch with reality during those times. However, I did cause some major problems by going on reckless buying sprees and spending huge amounts of money we didn't have. It ended up causing a lot of financial distress for myself and my family.\n\nDoctor: During those high periods, were you still able to function at work, school, and home, or did the symptoms make it impossible to keep up with your usual responsibilities?"

During those high periods, I actually felt the opposite of unable to function—I was hyperactive, starting multiple projects and doing far more work than usual. During those times, I ended up doing some really impulsive and risky things without thinking about the consequences.\n\nDoctor: Did those high periods ever cause major problems like needing hospitalization, losing touch with reality, or being unable to function at work or home?\n\nPatient: No, I never needed to be hospitalized or lost touch with reality during those times. However, I did cause some major problems by going on reckless buying sprees and spending huge amounts of money we didn't have. It ended up causing a lot of financial distress for myself and my family.\n\nDoctor: During those high periods, were you still able to function at work, school, and home, or did the symptoms make it impossible to keep up with your usual responsibilities?\n\nPatient: During those high periods, I actually felt the opposite of unable to function—I was hyperactive, starting multiple projects and doing far more work than usual. However, that high energy also led to impulsive decisions like reckless spending and making rash business investments. So it didn't stop me from keeping up with responsibilities, but I made some very foolish choices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like that that was so severe it caused marked impairment, psychosis, or required hospitalization?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it has ever been that severe. I've never been hospitalized, and I have never experienced anything like psychosis.\n\nDoctor: Based on your answers, the pattern is most consistent with Bipolar II Disorder rather than Bipolar I Disorder. Would you like me to explain why?\n\nPatient: Yes, please, I would really like to hear your explanation. How do my symptoms of elevated, irritable moods and these periods of low energy and loss of interest distinguish Bipolar II from Bipolar I?"
  }
]

Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).
